I end up shifting around so much that people look over at me. I don't really mean to.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I suppose that's actually why I made the appointment. I just... I can't seem to get anything in order anymore. \n\nMy kitchen table... it's just covered. And then... well, the whole day is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being more active than your peers?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. If anything, the other ladies I know seem to get a lot more done than I do. I'm not really... out and about very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really do things on impulse. I'm not one to rush into things.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: No... my memory seems fine. And then... well, the whole day is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being more active than your peers?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. If anything, the other ladies I know seem to get a lot more done than I do. I'm not really... out and about very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really do things on impulse. I'm not one to rush into things.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: No... my memory seems fine. I mean, I remember what I need to, mostly. My husband's appointment papers, flyers from church, notes from the senior center... half-finished lists. I just sit there looking at it all. I don't know which thing to pick up first. And then... well, the whole day is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being more active than your peers?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. If anything, the other ladies I know seem to get a lot more done than I do. I'm not really... out and about very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to think about something else?\n\nPatient: Well... no. I don't think so. I haven't really noticed anything like that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't really do things on impulse. I'm not one to rush into things.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that happened recently?\n\nPatient: No... my memory seems fine. I mean, I remember what I need to, mostly. I haven't really noticed any real trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain routines or rituals in a specific way to feel comfortable or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No... I don't really do anything like that. I don't have any rituals or... whatever you'd call them. Just normal habits, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong need to arrange things in a specific order or symmetry?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't pay much attention to how things are arranged. They just sit wherever I leave them, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself getting distracted by things around you?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. If, say, the television is on in the next room... or if two people start talking near me at the senior center. My mind just sort of... follows that instead. And then I'll just lose my place in whatever I was writing, or... planning.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're moving around more than you should be, even when you're trying to stay still?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. Even when I'm just trying to sit politely in a meeting... I catch myself moving. Rubbing my hands, or... tapping my foot. Maybe clicking a pen. I just shift around in my chair quite a bit. Enough that... well, people look over at me sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're 'on the go' or like you can't slow down?\n\nPatient: No... I wouldn't say that. I don't feel driven like a motor or anything. I just... I just have a hard time sitting completely still, like I said. But I don't feel like I'm rushing around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble keeping track of your belongings or often misplace things?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose I do. I'm forever hunting for things I just had in my hand. It's... well, it's frustrating. The car keys, my reading glasses... appointment cards. Even recipes for the church supper, or... the folder I need to take to the doctor's office. I just set them down and... they're gone.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with organization and staying focused interfering with your relationships or daily activities?\n\nPatient: I suppose so... yes. It's... well, it's not easy to talk about. I did have to give up leading a big event over at the senior center recently. And... things with my family. My daughter has started double-checking my bills. And my appointments. She just... I suppose she no longer trusts that I can keep everything straight on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with family or friends because of your difficulties with organization and focus?\n\nPatient: I... well, I suppose I just told you about my daughter. That's... that's the main thing. I don't really know what else to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any extremely stressful or traumatic events recently?\n\nPatient: No... no, I wouldn't say so. Nothing like that. Just... the usual things, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant decline in your ability to think, learn new things, or make decisions compared to the past few years?\n\nPatient: No... I don't believe so. My mind is fine. I can still understand things and... make decisions just like before. It's really just... well, what we already talked about. Nothing has really changed in that regard."
